Example 2

(Ring Protocol II)
.

Consider the global type that occurs during the ring protocol:

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{add}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{\mathsf{add}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\mathsf{add}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}\left\{{\mathsf{add}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}_{\text{ring}}}\right\}}\\
\mathsf{sub}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}\left\{{\mathsf{sub}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}_{\text{ring}}}\right\}}\end{array}}\right\}}}}}

(9)

We have that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{aRoles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{sRoles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
.

2.2
Projections

In the top-down approach of MPST, local types are obtained
by projecting a global type onto roles. Whereas projection is traditionally presented as a
partial function
from a global type and a participant to a local type
[
35
]
, our
Def.

4
below instead defines it as a
coinductive ternary relation

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})}}
between a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
, a participant
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, and a (queue type, local type) pair. It should be read as a
predicate
which holds when
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})
is a valid projection of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
at
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
. The queues
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}}
allow us to capture buffered messages from en-route transmissions at the local level.

The rules are organised by the relationship between the projected role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and the outermost interaction of
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
. When
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is the sender, its local type begins with an internal choice (
[P-
\oplus
]
); when
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is the receiver, with an external choice (
[P-
\&
]
); when
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is not involved, the continuations from each branch must be reconciled into a single local type using merge (
[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
]
). Each of these three cases has a corresponding rule (suffixed -II) that handles the additional presence of an en-route transmission in
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
, extracting the relevant queue prefix before proceeding. Finally,
[P-End]
and
[P-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}
]
cover termination.

Definition 4

(Global Type Projection)
.

The
projection relation

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})}}
(read “
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})
is a projection of the global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
onto the participant
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
”) is the largest relation such that whenever
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})}}
holds:

[P-End]

If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{aRoles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}

[P-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\notin{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{sRoles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}

[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
, then for all
i\in I

there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right)}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\}_{i\in I}}}\ni{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}

[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
-II]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}}\right\}}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\right)}}

[P-
\oplus
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
, then
for all
i\in I

there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right)}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}

[P-
\oplus
-II]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}}\right\}}}
, then
there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\right)}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\right)}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}}}}}}}

[P-
\&
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
, then
for all
i\in I

there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right)}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}

[P-
\&
-II]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}}\right\}}}
, then
there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\right)}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\right\}}

In rule
[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
]
, a role not involved in a choice cannot observe which branch was taken. The projection must therefore reconcile the continuations from each branch into a single local type that is compatible with all of them. This is the role of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{}}
, the
merge operation for session types
(
full merging
).

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{}}

is a partial function that takes
a finite and non-empty set of local types,
\mathcal{T}
,
and returns
a set of local types,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\mathcal{T}}}
.
We say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
is a merge of
\mathcal{T}

(equivalently,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
is a merge of the types in
\mathcal{T}
)
to mean that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\mathcal{T}}}
.
We say that
\mathcal{T}
is mergeable
(equivalently, the types in
\mathcal{T}
are mergeable)
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\mathcal{T}}}\neq\emptyset
.
In the case of indexed sets,
we may write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}}

for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\colon i\in I\}}}
.

We define the merge operation using the coinductive relation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}}
below,
by defining

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\mathcal{T}}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\mathcal{T}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right\rangle}\}
.
We define
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}}
to be
the largest relation between non-empty finite sets of local types
and local types such that if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\colon i\in I\}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right\rangle}
, then:

[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{}}\text{-}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
for all
i\in I

[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{}}\text{-}\oplus
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
, then for all
i\in I

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i,j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}

where for all
j\in J

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i,j}}\colon i\in I\right\}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}\right\rangle}

[
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{}}\text{-}\&
]

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\right\}_{j\in J}}
, then for all
i\in I

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S_{j}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i,j}}\right\}_{j\in J_{i}}}

where
J=\bigcup_{i\in I}J_{i}
and for all
j\in J

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i,j}}\colon j\in J_{i}\right\}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}}\right\rangle}

Here
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({\cdot}\right)}
is the unfolding function of
Def.

2
.

When the queue is empty,
we may leave it implicit i.e. if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})}}
then
we may instead write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}
.

The rule
[P-End]
says that if a global type doesn’t specify behaviour for some role, then its projection has no behaviour.
The rule
[P-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}
]
is similar, it says that if a role doesn’t add anything to the queue,
then its queue must be empty.
The rule
[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
]
says that if the head of the global type does not involve
a participant and
the projections of the continuations are compatible, then
the projection of the global type is their merge.
The rule
[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
-II]
says that
if the head of a global type is an en-route transmission not involving
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, then
the projection onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is the projection of the continuation.
The rule
[P-
\oplus
]
says that if the head of a global type is a communication with sender
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and destination
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
,
then the head of the projection onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is a send to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
with the same messages and payloads,
and the local continuations are the projections of the global continuations.
The rule
[P-
\oplus
-II]

allows an en-route message
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\right)}}
to be included in the projected queue of outgoing messages.
If a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
starts with a transmission from role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, projecting it onto
role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
(resp.
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
) results in an internal (resp. external) choice, provided that the continuation

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
is also projectable.
The rules
[P-&]
and
[P-&-II]
say that if the head of a global type is a communication with sender
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
and destination
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
then the head of the projection onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is a receive from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
with the same messages and payloads,
and the local continuations are the projections of the global continuations.
When projecting
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
onto other participants
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
(
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
), a merge operator, as defined in
Def.

4
,
is used to ensure that the projections of all continuations are “compatible”. It is noteworthy that there are global types that cannot be projected
onto all of their participants as shown by Udomsrirungruang and Yoshida
[
55
, §4.4]
.

We now return to our running example
to demonstrate a
derivation of
an instance of a projection and a merge.

Example 3

(Ring Protocol Projection)
.

Recall
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
from the ring-choice example (
§​

1.2
):

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{sub}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}\right\}}}}

(10)

We can derive

(11)

Noting that:

(12)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\right\}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{sub}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\right\}}\}},{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{sub}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\right\}}}\right\rangle}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\right\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[ (13)

Of course, this projection
is derivable by the full inductive projection,
which is a subrelation of the full coinductive projection;
this is not always the case.
We now provide a more involved
example,
which
makes use of en-route transitions
and cannot be projected inductively.

Example 4

(Coinductive Projection)
.

Consider the global type:

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{1}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{1}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}}}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{2}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{1}}}}\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}}}}}}}}}}}\end{array}}\right\}}

(14)

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
projects onto all three roles. The projections onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
are obtained directly from the coinductive rules without any merging.

(16)

Projecting onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is more delicate, because
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is not involved in the initial
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
choice and its continuations across the two branches differ. We isolate this case in the following example, in order to explain the merge step that reconciles the two continuations.

Example 5

(Merging in Projection)
.

Continuing from Example
4
, we project
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
. Since
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is not a participant in the top-level
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
communication, the applicable rule is
[P-
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqcap}}
]
: the projection is obtained by merging the projections of the two branches onto
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
. In each branch,
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is the sender of the en-route message from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
carrying label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}
, so
[P-
\oplus
-II]
applies and yields a projection of the form

]
, with one sub-obligation per label of the resulting external choice:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}\right\}}\}},{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}\end{array}\right\}}}\right\rangle}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}\}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}}\right\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.2344,0.3516,0.5508}\operatorname{merge}}\left\langle{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}\}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}}\right\rangle}

(19)

Intuitively, the merged type offers
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
both labels
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}
as an external choice from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
: under
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}
it continues as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}
does after unfolding, immediately re-entering the loop; under
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}
it continues as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}
does after its
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}2}}
-receive, which in this example also lands in the same loop
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}1}}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}}}
. Whichever label
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
eventually sends to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, the merged local type at
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
is ready for it, witnessing the compatibility of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{1}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{2}}
.

2.3
Asynchronous Multiparty Subtyping

Given a standard subtyping
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}
for basic types
(e.g. including
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{real}}}
),
we give a summary of
asynchronous subtyping

\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}
introduced in
[
29
]
. We first consider the tree representation
of local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
(denoted by
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)
).

We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}}
for generic trees and additionally define three specific types of tree.

Single-input
trees (denoted by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}}
) are those which have only a singleton choice in all branchings,
while
single-output
trees (denoted by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}
) are those which have only a singleton choice in all selections.
Trees which are both single-input and single-output are called
single-input-single-output (SISO) trees
(denoted by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}
).
These can all be defined coinductively by the following equations.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}

(20)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}

(21)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}

(22)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}

\displaystyle=\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}

(23)

We will define reorderings of SISO trees, and to do so, we consider non-empty sequences
\mathcal{A}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}
of receives not including
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

and
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}
of sends not including
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
together with receives from any participant.
These sequences are inductively defined (where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
) by:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathcal{A}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}&=\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{\mathcal{A}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}}\quad\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}\penalty\ =\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}}\end{array}

(24)

We define the set
\texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}})
of actions of a SISO tree:

\texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}})=\emptyset
;

\texttt{act}({\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}?\}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}})
; and

\texttt{act}({\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}})=\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}!\}\penalty\ \cup\penalty\ \texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}})
.
Using these definitions, we introduce a
refinement relation
(
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}
) defined coinductively by the following rules:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}\mathcal{A}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}\mathcal{A}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}^{\prime}\texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}})=\texttt{act}(\mathcal{A}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}^{\prime})\par\\[8.61108pt]
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}^{\prime}\texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}})=\texttt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}^{\prime})\par\\[8.61108pt]
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}^{\prime}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}^{\prime}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}\par\qquad\par{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}^{\prime}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}^{\prime}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}\par\qquad\par{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\end{array}

We can extract sets of single-input and single-output trees from a given tree using the functions
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
and
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\{{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}_{i}}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}\}\\[8.61108pt]
{\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}=\{{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}}\mid\forall i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}\}\quad{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\}\\[8.61108pt]
{\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\{{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}\}\\[8.61108pt]
{\llbracket{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}=\{{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}}\mid\forall i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{i}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}\}\quad{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}=\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\}\\[8.61108pt]
\end{array}

Definition 5

(Subtyping)
.

We consider trees that have only singleton choices in branchings (called
single-input (SI) trees
), or in selections (
single-output (SO) trees
), and we define the session subtyping
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}
over all session types by considering their decomposition into SI, SO, and SISO trees.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\lx@proof@logical@and\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}\forall{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}\exists{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}

(25)

The refinement
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}
captures
safe permutations
of input/output messages,
that never cause deadlocks or communication errors under asynchrony;
and the subtyping relation
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}
focuses on
reconciling refinement
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}
with the branching
structures in session types.

Example 6

(Subtyping the Ring Protocol Projection (
§​

1.2
))
.

Recall the local types:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{sub}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}\right\}}}}

(26)

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{sub}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\right\}}\right\}}}}

(27)

To demonstrate that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
, we must show that for all
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
, there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}
.
Consider the following sets:

\displaystyle{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{add}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{add}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{\ldots}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{sub}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{add}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{\ldots}},\ldots\right\}

(28)

\displaystyle{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{add}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\oplus\!\left\{{\begin{array}[]{l}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{add}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\ldots\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{sub}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\ldots\end{array}}\right\}}}\right\}

Now, we must find for each
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}
in the first set and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}
in the second, a pair of SISO trees
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}})
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}
.
For instance, if the second
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}
is chosen, we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{sub}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{add}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{\ldots}}
and we can pick

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{add}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathsf{sub}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}})};{\ldots}}

Then we can apply rule
[Ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
to validate that it is safe to reorder the send ahead of the receive in the optimised type.
We could form a similar argument in the other cases.
Thus we conclude that:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
.

Compactness of Subtypes.

Beyond enabling message reordering optimisations,
subtyping, in both synchronous and asynchronous settings,
can also potentially yield
arbitrarily more compact
type representations.
This compactness is particularly valuable in practical implementations,
where small types can also simplify readability and understanding.

For example, consider a type that reads exactly
n
messages labelled
\mathsf{a}
followed by one
\mathsf{b}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}=\underbrace{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\mathsf{a}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\mathsf{a}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\cdots{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\mathsf{a}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\mathsf{b}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\right\}}\right\}}\right\}}\right\}}}_{\text{$n$ nested external choices}}

(31)

This type has size
O(n)
. However, a subtype that accepts
any
number of
\mathsf{a}
’s before
\mathsf{b}
can be written compactly as:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{\mathsf{a}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}},\ \mathsf{b}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\right\}}}}

(32)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{n}}
for all
n
, yet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
has constant size
O(1)
.
This is in fact the case even for real projected local types, as coinductive projection
can in general yield local types superpolynomial in the size of the global type
[
55
]
.

Properties of Subtyping.

While asynchronous subtyping is very flexible,
allowing us to delay communications and
simplify structure,
this can not be done indefinitely.
For example, asynchronous subtyping never
admits a non-trivial supertype of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
.

Lemma 1

(End Subtyping)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
.

Proof.

The type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
is conveniently already a SISO tree. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
, by inversion we see that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
can only be derived from
[Ref-End]
,
and so it must be that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
.
∎

We also note that,
as one might expect from usual properties
of subtyping,
the precise asynchronous subtyping
is a preorder.

Lemma 2

(Reflexivity and Transitivity of Subtyping, Lemma 3.8 in Ghilezan et al.
[
29
]
)
.

For any closed, well-guarded local types
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}
: (1)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
holds, and (2) if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}
then it must be that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}}
holds.

However, unlike the synchronous subtyping (
Def.

5
),
the precise asynchronous subtyping
does not give us a strong operational
correspondence between instances.
This is elaborated further in
Ex.

15
.
Regardless, we retain a weak operational correspondence,
witnessed by
Lem.

16
’s structural inversion of subtyping.

2.4
Queue Subtyping

As we have the ground subtyping
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}

and ground types in our queues,
we can define a subtyping for queues.
This is important as
when we subtype local types we
may use
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}

to alter the payload types;
we will need to subtype projected
queues to identify an
operational correspondence after
local actions.
The queue subtyping is defined to be covariant
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}
i.e. we define the queue subtyping
inductively by:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{L}}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{R}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{L}}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{R}}}}}}}\lx@proof@logical@and{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{L}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{L}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{R}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{R}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\right)}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}})}\right)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}

The projection of a global type onto a participant produces a pairs containing
a queue and a local type.
Thus in order to use local type subtyping after projecting as in
Eq.

6
(
§​

1
),
we also require this notion of queue subtyping.

3
Operational Semantics of Global and Local Types

In this section, we will introduce semantics for global types
and typing contexts.
While the typing context semantics are standard
[
51
]
,
our global type semantics (
Def.

7
) is new
and has been designed to reflect
the typing context semantics up-to
the precise asynchronous subtyping.

3.1
Semantics of Global Types

We now present the Labelled Transition System (LTS) semantics for global types.
To begin, we introduce the transition labels in
Def.

6
,
which are also used in the LTS semantics of typing contexts (discussed later in
§​

3.2
).

Definition 6

(Transition Labels)
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}
be a transition label of the form:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}\ \Coloneqq\ {{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}\ \mathbin{\;\big|\;}\ {{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}\quad\text{(receive or send a message)}

(33)

The subject of a transition label, written

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}
,  is defined as follows:

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}})}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}})}={\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

(34)

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}
then we say that

{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}})}\preccurlyeq{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}

and

{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}\preccurlyeq{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}})}

The label

{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}

denotes that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
has enqueued a message
with label
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}
and payload of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}

onto its queue for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

and the label

{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}

denotes that
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
has dequeued/received a message
with label
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}
and payload of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}

from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
’s queue.

Definition 7

(Global Type Transitions)
.

The global type transition
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}
is inductively
defined by the following rules.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}{}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}/{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{t}}}]\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}}\right\}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}\\[8.61108pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}})}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{j}}}}}\right\}}}j\in I\\[8.61108pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\lx@proof@logical@and\forall i\in I:{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}_{i}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{subject}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}})}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\\[8.61108pt]
