(57)

This idea is illustrated through
two main theorems:

Thm.

12
shows that the reducibility of a global type aligns with that of
its associated typing context; while

Thm.

11
illustrates that each possible transition of a typing context is simulated by
an action in the transitions of the associated global type.
The following two subsections (
§​

5.1
and
5.2
)
are dedicated to the proofs of these results.

Theorem 11

(Completeness of Association)
.

Given
associated global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
and typing context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}

such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
,
then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}\preccurlyeq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 12

(Soundness of Association)
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
and assume

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.
Then there exist actions
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}\preccurlyeq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}
,
a context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
, and a global type
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime\prime}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime}}}}
.

Remark 4

(
Thms.

11
and
12
from the Perspective of Processes)
.

In
Thms.

29
and
31
,
we shall see that
Thms.

11
and
12

are analogues for subject reduction
and session fidelity, respectively,
with typing contexts and global types
taking the place of
sessions and typing contexts.
In both cases,
Thms.

11
and
12

bridge the gap between typing
sessions with contexts
and typing them with global types.

Remark 5

(Sufficiency of Soundness)
.

As in
[
36
]
,
our soundness condition necessitates
that we change our transition label.
However, unlike in the synchronous case,
we can no longer guarantee that the participants remain the same.
In the synchronous setting,
subtyping will allow for fewer selections
in associated contexts than in global types,
meaning that the global transition label
may use a selection label that is absent
locally, forcing us to change to a label that is present.
In the asynchronous setting this can still occur,
but now the asynchronous subtyping will
also prevent us from transitioning on the
same participants locally.
For example, if the global type has
a branching label from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

(corresponding to a head transition
and so reflected in the projected context),
then the asynchronous subtyping may have
moved a branching from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

to the head of the context’s type for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
,
preventing
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

from performing any action.
This behaviour can be witnessed below in
Eq.

58
.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}\right)}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}})}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}\right\}}\right\}})}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}\mathbin{\!:\!}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}\right\}})}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}\right\}}}\right\}}}}

(58)

Before we can use the association to
define the top-down typing system
as a bottom-up typing system,
we must ensure that associated contexts are
safe, deadlock-free, and live.

Theorem 13

(Associated Context Properties)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
is safe, deadlock-free, and live.

Section
5.3
is dedicated to the proof of this result.

5.1
Structure of Asynchronous Subtyping

The issue that we introduce by using asynchronous subtyping,
is that local asynchronous transitions, with participant
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, need not correspond
to the shallowest occurrences of
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

in the corresponding global type.
We follow Ghilezan et al.
[
28
]
to resolve this issue,
by introducing local and global type contexts,
where the holes correspond to
redexes that are reduced during
asynchronous communication.
However, our choice of global type transition has introduced non-determinism,
which we must resolve when identifying local transitions to global transitions.
Our solution is to introduce ‘forgetful holes’,
\langle\cdot\rangle_{i}
,
both locally and globally;
these holes will denote where the asynchronous subtyping
forgets selections,
so that we can identify which branches to forget
when making global transitions.

Remark 6

(Inductive Contexts over Coinductive Types)
.

The contexts defined below (
Defs.

22
and
24
)
are
inductively
defined and do not include a case
for recursive types.
This is because they operate on the
tree representations

\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)
and
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)
,
which are coinductive (potentially infinite) trees
obtained by fully unfolding all
\mu
-binders.
Since the tree representations contain no
\mu
-binders,
the context grammars need no corresponding
\mu
case.
The contexts themselves are always finite,
capturing only the structure between the root of the tree
and the finitely many redex positions,
while the (potentially infinite) subtrees
are used to fill in the holes.

Definition 22

(Global Type Contexts)
.

Analogously to Ghilezan et al.
[
28
, Def A.19]
, we
define global type contexts, with indexed holes, inductively as follows,
where we assume
({\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{b}}}})\neq({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})
,
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
, and
I_{R}\neq\emptyset
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}\

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{b}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{s}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}}}}\right\}}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ [\cdot]_{i}

\displaystyle\mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{b}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\langle\cdot\rangle_{l_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{L}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{b}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{R}}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}\

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{a}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{c}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}}}}\right\}}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ [\cdot]_{i}

We define separate contexts for situations in which it is safe to reduce transmissions (
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}
) and en-route transmissions (
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}
) respectively.
In either case, given
\mathbb{G}_{\star}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}

where
\star\in\{\oplus,\&\}
,
the context has some hole indexing set
I
and some forgetful hole indexing set
I^{\prime}
.
We require the indexing sets of continuations to be disjoint
and for
I
and
I^{\prime}
to be disjoint.
We write
\mathbb{G}_{\star}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}_{i}]_{i\in I}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}_{i}\rangle_{i\in I^{\prime}}
for the tree given by populating
the holes labelled with
i
by the global type tree
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}_{i}
for
i\in I

and the forgetful holes labelled with
i
by the global type tree
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}_{i}
for
i\in I^{\prime}
.

Of course, we will need to specify
the shape of a global type after
it performs an asynchronous transition.
Via
[GR-Ctx-I’]
, we can do this
by explicitly removing the choices that result
in a forgetful hole.

Definition 23

(Forgetting Forgetful Holes)
.

Given a context
\mathbb{G}
, we define
\overline{\mathbb{G}}
to be the context
where we have removed all of the forgetful holes from
\mathbb{G}
recursively:

•

\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\langle\cdot\rangle_{l_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{L}}}+{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}_{i}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{R}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\overline{\mathbb{G}_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{R}}}

•

\overline{[\cdot]_{i}}=[\cdot]_{i}

•

\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}}}}\right\}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\overline{\mathbb{G}}}}}\right\}}}

We now provide similar definitions for local type contexts,
so that we can observe asynchronous semantics
in projected types.

Definition 24

(Local Type Tree Context)
.

Define a local type tree context inductively as follows (
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}\

\displaystyle=\ [\cdot]_{i}\ \penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}\right\}_{i\in I}}\penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\langle\cdot\rangle_{l_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I_{L}}}+{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}\right\}_{i\in I_{R}}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}\

\displaystyle=\ [\cdot]_{i}\ \penalty\ \mid\penalty\ {\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}\right\}_{i\in I}}

We define separate contexts for situations in which it is safe to reduce transmissions (
\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}
) and en-route transmissions (
\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}
) respectively.
In either case, given
\mathbb{L}_{\star}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}

where
\star\in\{\oplus,\&\}
,
the context has some hole indexing set
I
and some forgetful hole indexing set
I^{\prime}
.
We require the indexing sets of continuations to be disjoint
and for
I
and
I^{\prime}
to be disjoint.
We write
\mathbb{L}_{\star}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}_{i}]_{i\in I}\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}_{i}\rangle_{i\in I^{\prime}}
for the tree given by populating
the holes labelled with
i
by the local type tree
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}_{i}
for
i\in I

and the forgetful holes labelled with
i
by the local type tree
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}_{i}
for
i\in I^{\prime}
.

Before:
\mathbb{L}_{\oplus}^{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\oplus\{\cdots\}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\mathbin{\&}\{\cdots\}
[\cdot]_{1}
\langle\cdot\rangle_{2}
add
sub
add
(tracked)
(forgotten)
\overline{\mathbb{L}}
After:
\overline{\mathbb{L}}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\oplus\{\cdots\}
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\mathbin{\&}\{\cdots\}
[\cdot]_{1}
add
add
sub
branch
removed

Figure 4
:
Local type context with normal and forgetful holes for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}
from the ring protocol.

Left:
The context where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
selects a branch to send to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
.
The
add
branch leads to a normal hole
[\cdot]_{1}
(tracked continuation),
while
sub
leads to a forgetful hole
\langle\cdot\rangle_{2}
(to be forgotten).

Right:
After applying
\overline{\mathbb{L}}
, only the
add
branch remains.

Definition 25

(Forgetting Forgetful Holes Locally)
.

Given a context
\mathbb{L}
, we define
\overline{\mathbb{L}}
to be the context
where we have removed all of the forgetful holes from
\mathbb{L}
recursively:

•

\overline{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\langle\cdot\rangle_{l_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I_{L}}}+{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I_{R}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\overline{\mathbb{L}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I_{R}}}

•

\overline{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\overline{\mathbb{L}_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}

•

\overline{[\cdot]_{i}}=[\cdot]_{i}

Our aim is to use local contexts to
determine redexes for the asynchronous subtyping,
to identify them in global contexts.
Therefore, it will be necessary to
link global contexts with local contexts in some way.
Taking inspiration from our types,
we provide a partial projection function.
Note that as en-route transmissions
induced by a global transition may occur
both
in front of some global redexes and
following some global redexes,
we must keep track of the queue up to
each hole, instead
of requiring uniformity of the queues.

Definition 26

(Context Projection)
.

We define a partial function from global type contexts
with indexed holes to
queues indexed by the global holes and local type contexts,
written
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{i}}}\}_{i\in I},\mathbb{L})

with indexes being sets of indexes from the global context,
inductively as follows:

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}[\cdot]_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\{i\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}\},[\cdot]_{\{i\}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\langle\cdot\rangle_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\{i\mapsto{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}\},\langle\cdot\rangle_{\{i\}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}_{i}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\bigcup_{i\in I}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{1},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{2}\right\}_{i\in I}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}}}\right\}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{1},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{2}\right\}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}_{i}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\bigcup_{i\in I}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{1},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{2}\right\}_{i\in I}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}}}\right\}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\right)}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{1}(j)}}}}\}_{j},{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{2}\right\}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}_{i}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\bigcup_{i\in I}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}_{i}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{2}}})

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}}}\right\}}}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{1},({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}^{\prime}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}})_{2})

where we define the merge of contexts, where the hole indexes are sets of indexes, by

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{I\in\mathcal{I}}{[\cdot]_{I}}}=[\cdot]_{\{i\colon i\in I\in\mathcal{I}\}}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{I\in\mathcal{I}}{\langle\cdot\rangle_{I}}}=\langle\cdot\rangle_{\{i\colon i\in I\in\mathcal{I}\}}

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{I\in\mathcal{I}}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}_{I,j}\right\}_{j\in J_{I}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\{\mathbb{L}_{I,j}\colon j\in J_{I}\}}}\right\}_{j\in J}}

where
J=\bigcup_{I\in\mathcal{I}}J_{I}
and
\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}\}_{j\in J}
are distinct

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{I\in\mathcal{I}}{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}\mathbb{L}_{I,j}\right\}_{j\in J}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{I\in\mathcal{I}}{\mathbb{L}_{I,j}}}\right\}_{j\in J}}

The definition of context projections and context merges,
follow the structure of type projections and type merges.
The exception being that, in the context case,
we will not determine the entire queue before reaching a hole
so we must remember the queue upto each hole.

Now to make use of the context projection,
we observe that it indeed
corresponds to type projections.

Lemma 14

(Context Merges correspond to Merges)
.

Suppose that for
i\in I
,
\mathbb{L}_{i}

are local contexts where the hole indexes are
disjoint sets.
Say
\mathbb{L}_{i}
has hole index set
J_{i}
and
forgetful hole index set
J^{\prime}_{i}
.
Suppose that
\mathbb{L}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{\mathbb{L}_{i}}}
,
with hole indexes
J
and forgetful hole indexes
J^{\prime}
.
Suppose that
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)=\mathbb{L}[\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}}\right)]_{j\in J}\langle\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}}\right)\rangle_{j\in J^{\prime}}
,
for all
i\in I

\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}}\right)=\mathbb{L}_{i}[\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i,j}}}\right)]_{j\in J_{i}}\langle\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i,j}}}\right)\rangle_{j\in J^{\prime}_{i}}
, and
for all
j\in J

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap{\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i,j^{\prime}}}\colon i\in I,j^{\prime}\in J_{i}\cup J^{\prime}_{i},j^{\prime}\subseteq j\}}}\ni{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}}}\ni{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
.

Proof.

By induction on local contexts.
∎

Lemma 15

(Context Projections correspond to Projections)
.

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbb{G}}\!\upharpoonright\!{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}=(\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{i}}}\}_{i\in I\cup J},\mathbb{L})

with index sets
I\cup J
and
I^{\prime}\cup J^{\prime}
, respectively.
Suppose that
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)=\mathbb{G}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}]_{i\in I}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle_{i\in J}
,

\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)=\mathbb{L}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}]_{i\in I^{\prime}}\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}\rangle_{i\in J^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{i}}=\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}\right)
for
i\in I\cup J
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}=\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\right)
for
i\in I^{\prime}\cup J^{\prime}
, and
for
i\in I\cup J
there is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}_{i}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{i}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}_{i}})}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\bigsqcap_{i\in J}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime\prime}_{i}}}}\ni{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{J}}
for
J\in I^{\prime}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{i}}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{i}}}}}}}
for
i\in I
.
Then,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on global contexts, using
Lem.

14

∎

With all of our tools in place,
we can now see how local contexts
and global contexts
can be used to capture
redexes up to
asynchronous subtyping.
Asynchronous subtyping allows redexes to move,
thus the top-level redex of an asynchronous subtype
appears somewhere in its supertype.

Lemma 16

(Inversion of subtyping)
.

(1)

If
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
then
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)=\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{i,j}}\right\}_{i\in I_{j}}}]_{j\in J}\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\rangle_{j\in J^{\prime}}

with
I\subseteq I_{j}
for all
j\in J
and for all
i\in I
and
j\in J
,
we have
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}
,
and if
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}}\right)=\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{i,j}}]_{j\in J}\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\rangle_{j\in J^{\prime}}

then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}
.

(2)

If
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
,
then
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)=\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{i,j}}\right\}_{i\in I_{j}}}]_{j\in J}

with
I_{j}\subseteq I
for all
j\in J
,

I=\bigcup_{j\in J}I
,
for all
j\in J
and
i\in I

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}
, and
if
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}}\right)=\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{i,j}}]_{j\in J}
for some
i\in\bigcap_{j\in J}I_{j}
then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{i}}
.

Proof.

We only show item (1) (as item (2) is dual).
Write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
and assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
.

By
Def.

5
, for every
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
and every
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}
.
The clause for
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}S}
yields, for each
i\in I
, an element

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}^{\prime}_{i}}}\quad\text{with}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}^{\prime}_{i}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}.

Fix
i\in I
and an arbitrary
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\in{\llbracket\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
. The subtyping premise gives SISO trees
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}_{i}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}}_{i}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}_{i}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}}_{i}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}_{i}
.
Unfolding
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}_{i}}
forces
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}_{i}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}}
for some
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{U}^{\prime}_{i}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SI}}
.
Invert the last rule of the refinement derivation of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}
.
Because
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}_{i}}
is headed by an output, the final rule is either
[Ref-Out]
or
[Ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
.

•

[Ref-Out]
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}^{\prime}_{i}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}
and premise
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
.
Then we must have that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I^{\prime}}}
with
I\subseteq I^{\prime}
.

•

[Ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
:
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}=\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}})};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}}^{\prime}_{i}}
for some non-empty finite
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}
,
\texttt{act}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}})=\texttt{act}(\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime\prime}_{i}})
, and premise
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\lesssim}}\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})};{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime\prime}_{i}}
.

For
[Ref-
\mathcal{B}
]
, write
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}=a;\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\mathrm{tail}}
with
a
the first action. We reason by case analysis on
a
, using that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
and the definitions of
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
(where
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}
and
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}
are the single-branch forms of internal and external choice, respectively):

•

If
a={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}^{\prime}})}
, then
{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
can contain such a prefix only if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{k}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}_{k}}\right\}_{k\in K}}
with some
k
satisfying
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{k}}}}
; moreover
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}_{k}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
.

•

If
a={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}^{\prime}})}
, with
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, and then
{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
can contain such a prefix only if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{k}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}_{k}}\right\}_{k\in K}}
with some
k
satisfying
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{k}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}^{\prime}
, and again
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{W}^{\prime}_{i}}\in{\llbracket{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{V}^{\prime}_{k}}\rrbracket}_{\text{SO}}
.

In either subcase we strip the head constructor of
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)
, using forgetful holes to cover any branches not in
I
,
obtaining a continuation, yielding a shorter prefix
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\mathrm{tail}}
. The induction hypothesis on
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\mathrm{tail}}
yields a decomposition in which every branch reached after
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\mathrm{tail}}
still contains an internal choice by
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
whose branch-set includes all labels in
I
with payloads extending the corresponding
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}
.
Re-wrapping the stripped constructor
a
preserves this property for the continuation, showing that along every branch of
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)
compatible with
\mathcal{B}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}
there is an occurrence of
{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{i,j}}\right\}_{i\in I_{j}}}
with
I\subseteq I_{j}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}
.
So the overall shape of
\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)
is
\mathbb{L}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\oplus}[{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,j}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime}_{i,j}}\right\}_{i\in I_{j}}}]_{j\in J}\langle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}^{\prime\prime}_{j}}\rangle_{j\in J^{\prime}}
as claimed.
∎

Recall the context rules

[GR-Ctx-I]
,

[GR-Ctx-I’]
and

[GR-Ctx-II]
from

Def.

7
;
these exactly say that
sends occur under
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}
contexts
and receives occur under
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}
contexts.

Lemma 17

(Global Type Tree Context Transitions)
.

1.

Let
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}
be a context with index set
J
and
forgetful index set
J^{\prime}
.
Let
k\in\bigcap_{j\in J}I_{j}
.
We have that

\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i,j}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{j}}}]_{j\in J}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}_{j}}\rangle_{j\in J^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{k}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{k}}}})}}}\overline{\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}}[{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{k,j}}}]_{j\in J}
.

2.

Let
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}
be a context with index set
J
.
We have that

\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})}_{\&}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}}}\right\}}}]_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}}}\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}[{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}}]_{j\in J}

Proof.

The proof in each case is by induction on the structure of the context.

1.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}
.

•

Case
\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}=[\cdot]
:
Immediate from
[GR-
\oplus
]
.

•

Case

•
