to get that

\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)=\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i,f(j)}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i,f(j)}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i,j}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I_{f(j)}}}]_{j\in K}\langle{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime\prime\prime}_{j}}\rangle_{j\in K^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}{}^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{k,j}}]_{j\in K}
for
k\in I
.
Then we use the additional conclusions
to reconstruct the subtyping derivation from before,
with updated premises taken from the results of applying the lemma,
to deduce that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}}}
where

\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{k}}}\right)=\mathbb{L}_{\oplus}{}^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbb{T}_{k,j}}]_{j\in J}
and

\operatorname{\mathfrak{T}}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\right)=\mathbb{G}^{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})}_{\oplus}{}^{\prime}[{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{k,j}}]_{j\in K}
.

2.

(Similar to previous case).

3.

From the projection rules and applying
Lem.

1
.

∎

See
11

Proof.

By case analysis on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}
.

Case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}={{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}

(a send by a participant).

Applying
Lem.

18
, for some label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}
,
payload
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}
, sender
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

and receiver
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\displaystyle\Delta}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}})

\displaystyle\text{with }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\text{ where }\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}.

We also know the endpoint at
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
is updated in the new context,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\right)}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}),

while all other endpoints stay unchanged.
As we know
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,
and so by association there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}})}}\quad\text{with}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\;\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\leqslant}}_{\text{a}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}

(59)

Hence we can apply
Lem.

22
to obtain the desired result.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}})}\right)}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}})}}\quad\text{with}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}\;\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}

(60)

And so, by also applying
Lem.

21
,we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}})}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
in this case.

Case
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}={{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}})}

(receive by a participant).

Dually to the above case, we can again apply
Lem.

18
, for some label
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}
,
payload
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}
, sender
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

and receiver
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
. Then we have

\begin{split}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}})\quad\text{and}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}})}\right)}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}})\\
&\text{with }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}\text{ where }\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}j}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}},\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}.\end{split}

(61)

Now the endpoints at
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
are updated, so

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}),

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}),

while other endpoints in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
stay the same.
Again, given
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}
, there exists
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}})}}\quad\text{with}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\;\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}.\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\leqslant}}_{\text{a}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}.

(62)

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime\prime}}}}})}\right)}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}})}}\quad\text{with}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}\;\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}\quad\text{and}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{<}{:}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime\prime}}}}\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\leqslant}}_{\text{a}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}.

(63)

Hence we can apply
Lem.

22
to obtain the desired result.

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}})}}\quad\text{with}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}\;\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}\;{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{j}}.

(64)

and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}})}}.

(65)

And so we have

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B^{\prime\prime}}}})}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.
∎

Now that we have
Thm.

11
,

Thm.

12
is truly quite weak.
Indeed, we only need to know that
contexts, associated to a non-
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
global type,
can move.

Lemma 23

(Projection preserves operational semantics)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}\supseteq{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{}}}
.

Proof.

If
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{\phantom{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathbf{end}}
so
write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
or

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
, then
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})
so

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
so

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}}}}
for
j\in I
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
, then
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}})
so

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}})}\right)}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}}}}}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\right)}={\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}i}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}
so

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{j}}}}}}
.
∎

See
12

Proof.

By association, we know there exists a context
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}}\right)}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then we can apply
Lem.

23
to get that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}
.
So,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}}}}
as
the precise asynchronous subtyping relates non-deadlocked contexts to non-deadlocked contexts.
Then the desired result is obtained by applying
Thm.

11
.
∎

5.3
Properties of Associated Contexts

Now only
Thm.

13
remains to be shown.
Similarly to the synchronous setting,
liveness implies deadlock-freedom.
However unlike in the synchronous setting,
liveness also implies safety.
Therefore, it suffices to prove that
associated contexts are live.
With the observation that liveness is downward closed under
the precise asynchronous subtyping,
we need only focus on projected contexts.

Lemma 24

(Liveness is Downwards closed under subtyping
[
29
, Lemma 4.10]
)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
is live and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
is live.

Now, the precise asynchronous subtyping cannot cause any further issues.
The proof that a projected context is live is very similar
to the proofs in the inductive case and in the synchronous setting.
We have already observed sufficient operational correspondence
between global types and projected contexts to derive liveness.

Lemma 25

(Standard Association is Complete)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
, then
there exist
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}\succcurlyeq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha^{\prime}}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Follows the same structure as the proof of
Thm.

11
.
∎

Lemma 26

(Standard Association is Head-Sound)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}

for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
, then:

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\to}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}_{i}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}}}}\right\}}_{i\in I}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\mathord{\oplus}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}k}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{k}}}}})}}}}}

for some
k\in I
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{unf}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}={\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}{\overset{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}{\rightsquigarrow}}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}{:}\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\mathtt{m}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})\vphantom{x}\!\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}.}}\!{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}}\right\}}}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\,\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\xrightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathord{:}{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}\&{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{m}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}}}}}
.

Proof.

Clear from the definition of projection.
∎

With this operational correspondence,
we can proceed normally.
We take fair sequences from a projected context
and use
Lem.

25
to
determine a corresponding sequence of global types.
By
Lem.

26
and fairness,
every global type head must be reduced.
Finally by
\text{balanced}^{+}
, we deduce that
every redex will either be reduced or,
eventually, become the head of the global type,
and then be reduced.

Lemma 27

(Projection ensures Liveness)
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{roles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}})}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,
then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
is live.

Proof.

Let
\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{n}}\right)_{n\in N}
with
\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}_{n}\right)_{n,n+1\in N}
be a fair sequence starting from
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
.
By
Lem.

25
, find

\left({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{n}}\right)_{n\in N}
(
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{0}}
=
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}
) and

\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}_{n}}\right)_{n\in N}
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}_{0}}
=
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
) such that

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{n}}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}_{n}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{n}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}_{n}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{n}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\alpha}_{n}}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{n+1}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{i}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}
has a message for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
in its queue, then

({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}})\in{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{mRoles}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}})}
so

\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{mDepth}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}})
exists.
Proceed by induction on
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{mDepth}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}})
.
Suppose that this message is never dequeued.
Now apply
Lem.

26
via fairness to get to
j>i
and
Lem.

4
by our assumption.
The message is still on the queue by our assumption, and
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{mDepth}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{j}}})<\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{mDepth}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}})
.
By the I.H, the message will be dequeued.
The case for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta_{i}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}
having a receive at the head is similar,
but we proceed by induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{depth}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}})}
instead of
\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\operatorname{mDepth}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{i}}})
.
∎

See
13

Proof.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}{\upharpoonright_{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}\!\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\right)}}}
for all
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
, by association.
By
Lem.

27
and
Lem.

24
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
is live so
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
is live.
∎

6
Deriving the Main Theorems from Associations

This section demonstrates how to derive the main theorems
using soundness and completeness of the associations, together with
the corresponding results from Ghilezan et al.
[
29
, Theorems 4.11, 4.12 and
4.13]
.

6.1
Asynchronous Multiparty Session Processes

We summarise the calculus from Ghilezan et al.
[
29
]
and
main properties.

Process Syntax.

We define
processes
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i},\ldots
) by the following grammar:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\ ::=\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\ell\langle e\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}\textstyle\sum_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{i}(x_{i})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}

where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\ell\langle e\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
is a
send
(selection) to role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
with label
\ell
and payload expression
e
,
followed by continuation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}
;

\sum_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{i}(x_{i})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i}
is a
receive
(branching) from role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
,
where a message with label
\ell_{k}
binds variable
x_{k}
and continues as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{k}
;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}
is a conditional;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
is the inactive process;

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}
are for recursion.

Queue Syntax.

We define
message queues
(
h,h_{i},\ldots
) by the following grammar:

h\ ::=\ \ \epsilon\mathbin{\;\big|\;}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},\ell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))\mathbin{\;\big|\;}h_{1}\cdot h_{2}

where
\epsilon
is the empty queue;

({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},\ell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))
is the queue denoting that the message with
label
\ell
and value
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}

has been sent to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
; and

h_{1}\cdot h_{2}
is the concatenation
of the queues
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
.
We consider message queues up to
an equivalence relation
\equiv
,
similar to
Def.

1

but with
\epsilon
for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}
,
values for basic types,
and message queues for syntactic queue types.

Remark 7

(Session Interleaving and Delegation)
.

This calculus excludes session interleaving
and delegation elements.
This choice was made by Ghilezan et al.
[
29
]
,
to simplify the calculus by Coppo et
al.
[
15
]

and focus on subtyping.
For more detail and examples of how to tackle this,
see the work by Coppo et al.
[
16
]
and
van den Heuvel and Pérez
[
56
]
.

Typing Judgements.

We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}
to denote that process
P
has local type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}

under process variable environment
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}
,
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}}}
to denote that queue
h
has queue type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}}}
.
The typing rules for processes are standard and are shared by both the top-down and
bottom-up systems. We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}
for the process-variable and expression-variable context,
mapping process variables to their local types and expression variables to their sorts.

[T-
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}
]
types the inactive process;
[T-Var]
and

[T-Rec]
allow recursion at the process level;
[T-
\oplus
]
and

[T-
\&
]
allow typing of selections and branchings;
[T-Cond]

types conditionals; and the subsumption rule
[T-Sub]
allows for subtyping.
Queue rules type the empty queue, single messages, and concatenation.
We assume a separate sorting system for expressions, at least providing
judgements of the form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{bool}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{int}}}}
for
boolean and integer expressions.

Definition 27

(Typing Rules for Processes
[
29
]
)
.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{end}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}\\[12.91663pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\ell_{j}\langle e_{j}\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{j}}\triangleright{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\oplus\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{\ell_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e_{j}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{j}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{j}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{j}}}\quad j\in I\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textstyle\sum_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{i}(x_{i})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i}}\triangleright{{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\&\!\left\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathtt{\ell_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}})}\mathbin{\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}.}\!}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}\right\}_{i\in I}}}\forall i\in I\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta},x_{i}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B_{i}}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{i}}}\\[12.91663pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{1}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{2}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}e}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\operatorname{bool}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{1}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{2}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Theta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}:{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\mathbf{t}}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}}\end{array}

The typing rules for queues are:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\epsilon}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\varnothing}}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},\ell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{\left({{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}},{{\ell}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}})}\right)}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{B}}}}\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{1}\cdot h_{2}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{1}}}}\mathbin{\!\cdot\!}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{2}}}}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{1}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{1}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{2}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.85}{{\sigma}_{2}}}}

The subsumption rule
[T-Sub]
can be explained by Liskov and Wing’s substitution principle
[
43
]
:
if
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}
is a subtype of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
,
then an object of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T}

can always replace an object of type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T^{\prime}}
.

Example 16

(Ring Protocol Processes)
.

Returning to the ring-choice protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{\tiny ring}}}

from
§​

1.2
,
a set of processes implementing the protocol can be given as follows:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathsf{add}\langle n\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}\left({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathsf{add}(x)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}+{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathsf{sub}(x)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\right)}}

(66)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathsf{add}\langle m\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathsf{add}(y)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathsf{sub}\langle m\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathsf{add}(y)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}}}

(67)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathsf{add}(z)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathsf{add}\langle z{+}k\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}+{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\mathsf{sub}(z)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\mathord{!}\mathsf{sub}\langle z{-}k\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}}

(68)

Processes
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}
are typed by the
corresponding projected local types from
§​

1.2
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}}}}
.

Observe that,
as far as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
is concerned,

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
’s outgoing message to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}

need not depend on the value
received from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.
A programmer can therefore write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
to send to
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}

before
receiving from
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
,
allowing the
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{r}}}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\to{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
communications
to proceed concurrently,
improving throughput
as illustrated in
Fig.

3
(b).
The projected type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
requires the receive first,
but
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
is typed by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}
,
and since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}^{\text{\tiny opt}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
,
the subsumption rule
[T-Sub]
accepts
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}

in place of any process typed by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}}}
.
In this way, the global protocol
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G_{\text{\tiny ring}}}

guarantees safety, deadlock-freedom, and liveness for the system,
while
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}
gives the programmer the freedom
to choose a more efficient implementation for
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}
.

Figure
5
highlights the bottom-up workflow that we use in this section:
local types are jointly model-checked against the property
\phi
and guide the typing of the programs.

Property
\varphi
model-check
(
\models
)

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

:
Bottom-up methodology for multiparty session types.
Processes

,

Asynchronous Multiparty Sessions.

We define
asynchronous multiparty session
(
M,M_{i},...
) as:

M\ ::=\ \ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\ |\ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathbin{\;\big|\;}M\ |\ M^{\prime}

where
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
denotes a process
P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}

plays as a role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
.

The structural precongruence relation
\Rrightarrow
used in

Def.

28
below allows us to disregard inactive processes,
unfolds recursion, and is closed under reflexivity and transitivity:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft\epsilon\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}\qquad\mathcal{M}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}\\[9.47217pt]
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\Rrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mu{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}.{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}\}\qquad\mathcal{M}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{M}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\quad\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\end{array}

Definition 28

(Reduction relation on sessions (asynchronous))
.

\begin{array}[]{l@{\;\;}lcl}{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Send]}}&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\ell\langle e\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}&\to&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\mathbin{\cdot}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},\ell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}\quad(e\downarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v})\\[3.50006pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Rcv]}}&\begin{aligned} {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft\textstyle\sum_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{i}(x_{i})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i}&\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\\
&\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},\ell_{k}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))\mathbin{\cdot}h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}\end{aligned}&\to&\begin{aligned} {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{k}\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}/x_{k}\}&\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\\
&\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}\end{aligned}\quad(k\in I)\\[3.50006pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Cond-T]}}&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}&\to&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}\quad(e\downarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{{{true}}}})\\[3.50006pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Cond-F]}}&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}&\to&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}\quad(e\downarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{{{false}}}})\\[3.50006pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[R-Struct]}}&\mathcal{M}_{1}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{1}\quad\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{2}\quad\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{2}\Rrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{2}&\Longrightarrow&\mathcal{M}_{1}\to\mathcal{M}_{2}\\[3.50006pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[Err-Mism]}}&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft\textstyle\sum_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{i}(x_{i})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{i}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},\ell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))\mathbin{\cdot}h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}&\to&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}\quad(\forall i\in I.\ \ell_{i}\neq\ell)\\[3.50006pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.1,0.3,0.1}\textsc{\scriptsize[Err-Eval]}}&{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;\mathcal{M}&\to&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}\quad(e\ndownarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{{{true}}}}\text{ and }e\ndownarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\text{{{false}}}})\end{array}

Safety and Deadlock-Freedom.

We first define safety and deadlock-freedom for multiparty sessions.

Definition 29

(Safety and Deadlock-Freedom)
.

A session
M
is:

1.

communication safe
iff for all
M^{\prime}
such that
M\to^{\ast}M^{\prime}
, we have
M^{\prime}\not\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\mathtt{err}}}
;

2.

deadlock-free
iff for all
M^{\prime}
such that
M\to^{\ast}M^{\prime}
and
M^{\prime}\not\to
,
we have
M^{\prime}\equiv\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi_{i\in I}}{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{i}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{0}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}_{i}\triangleleft\epsilon)}}
.

Intuitively, safety ensures that no reachable session can reduce to an error state.
Deadlock-freedom ensures that if a session cannot reduce further,
then all processes have terminated and all queues are empty.

Session Liveness.

Liveness is a stronger property that subsumes both safety and deadlock-freedom (recall
Defs.

10
,
11
and
12
for the corresponding context-level properties).
We define liveness for multiparty sessions, ensuring that
under fair scheduling, all pending actions are eventually performed.
A
session path
is a (potentially infinite) sequence of sessions
(M_{i})_{i\in I}
,
where
I=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
is a set of consecutive natural numbers,
and for all
i\in I
,
M_{i}\to M_{i+1}
.

Definition 30

(Fair and Live Session Paths
[
29
]
)
.

A session path
(M_{i})_{i\in I}
is
fair
iff, for all
i\in I
:

SF1

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\ell\langle e\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}
,
then
\exists k
such that
I\ni k\geq i
and
M_{k}\to M_{k+1}

via
[R-Send]
at role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

SF2

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft\textstyle\sum_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{j}(x_{j})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{j}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}

and
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\triangleleft({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}},\ell_{k}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))\mathbin{\cdot}h^{\prime}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime\prime}
for some
k\in J
,
then
\exists k^{\prime}
such that
I\ni k^{\prime}\geq i
and
M_{k^{\prime}}\to M_{k^{\prime}+1}

via
[R-Rcv]
at role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

SF3

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}
,
then
\exists k
such that
I\ni k\geq i
and
M_{k}\to M_{k+1}

via
[R-Cond-T]
or
[R-Cond-F]
at role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

A session path
(M_{i})_{i\in I}
is
live
iff, for all
i\in I
:

SL1

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{if}\,{e}\,\mathbf{then}}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\,\mathbf{else}\,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}
,
then
\exists k
such that
I\ni k\geq i
and, for some
M^{\prime\prime}
,

M_{k}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime\prime}
or

M_{k}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime\prime}

SL2

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{!}\ell\langle e\rangle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}
,
then
\exists k
such that
I\ni k\geq i
and
M_{k}\to M_{k+1}

via
[R-Send]
at role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}

SL3

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}},\ell({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}v}))\mathbin{\cdot}h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}
,
then
\exists k
such that
I\ni k\geq i
and
M_{k}\to M_{k+1}

via
[R-Rcv]
at role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}

SL4

if
M_{i}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft\textstyle\sum_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{q}}}}}\mathord{?}\ell_{j}(x_{j})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\!.\!}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}_{j}\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h\;\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mid}}\;M^{\prime}
,
then
\exists k,\ell^{\prime}
such that
I\ni k\geq i
,
\ell^{\prime}\in\{\ell_{j}\}_{j\in J}
, and

M_{k}\to M_{k+1}
via
[R-Rcv]
at role
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}
receiving label
\ell^{\prime}

A session
M
is
live
iff all fair paths starting from
M
are live.

Intuitively, fairness (
SF1
–
SF3
) requires that if a component (process or queue)
is ready to fire an action, then that action will eventually be performed.
Liveness (
SL1
–
SL4
) then ensures that under fair execution:
conditionals are eventually resolved (
SL1
),
pending outputs are eventually performed (
SL2
),
queued messages are eventually received (
SL3
),
and pending inputs eventually receive a message (
SL4
).

6.2
Deriving the Main Theorems

We recall the bottom-up typing system for a multiparty session:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny bot}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({\Delta}\right)}}{\ ({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\ |\ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}})\ }}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({\Delta}\right)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\triangleright{\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})=(\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}})\quad\varphi({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta})

where

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}
states that
process
P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
has type
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\triangleright{\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}
types message queue

h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
has a queue type
\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}
, and

\varphi
is some desired property, which is usually a

safety
property–a selected label is always available at the
branching process

[
51
,
36
]
.
In the work of Ghilezan et al.
[
29
]
, a
liveness
property
[
29
, Definition 4.17]
is used instead for proving the preciseness of

\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}
. To derive the main theorems,
we do not require the details of typing rules, hence we omit.
See Ghilezan et al.
[
29
, § 7.1]
for the full typing system and its explanations.

Deriving Subject Reduction Theorem.

We prove the subject reduction theorem of the top-down system using
the completeness of the association with
the following subject reduction theorem of the bottom-up system.

Theorem 28

(Subject Reduction,
[
29
, Theorem 4.11]
)
.

Assume

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny bot}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}}
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
live and

M\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\to}}}{}^{\!\!\!*}}}}M^{\prime}
.
Then there exist live
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny bot}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M^{\prime}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{}^{\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
.

Theorem 29

(Subject Reduction of the Top-Down System)
.

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny top}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
, and

M\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\to}}}{}^{\!\!\!*}}}}M^{\prime}
.
Then there exists
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny top}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M^{\prime}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,\,\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{}}{}^{\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}*}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}
.

Proof.

We prove the following stronger statement, from which we can derive the
above theorem

Assume
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny top}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}
, and

M\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\to}}}{}^{\!\!\!*}}}}M^{\prime}
.
Then there exist
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny top}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M^{\prime}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}}

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\leqslant_{\text{a}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{}^{\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}\,\,\,\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}\xrightarrow{}}{}^{\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}*}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
, and

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}
.

Assume
M\equiv\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({\Delta}\right)}}{({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\ |\ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}})}

and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny top}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}

is derived with

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\operatorname{dom}\!\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\right)}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}h_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\triangleright{\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}})=(\sigma_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}T_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}})\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}}

(69)

by
[SessST]
.
Suppose
M\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\to}}}M^{\prime}
.
We need to prove that there exist
{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
such that

\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{role}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}\right)}\ ({\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}\ |\ {\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\triangleleft h^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}})

with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\sqsubseteq}_{a}}{{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G^{\prime}}}}
.

Note that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta}
is live by
Thm.

13
.
Hence by
Thm.

28
,
there exist live
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash^{\text{\tiny bot}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\Pi_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}\in\operatorname{role}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}G}}\right)}\ {P^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}{\boldsymbol{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0}\mathtt{p}}}}}}}}}\triangleright{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}}}

.
Then by

,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\to}}{}^{\!{\color[rgb]{0.43,0.21,0.1}*}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.9}\Delta^{\prime}}
